\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-s}h_{s,i}(X_s)
-\E\left(\left.\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-s}h_{s,i}(X_s)
\right|\hbox{ location arrays}\right)
\right|^4
\right|\hbox{ location arrays}
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
C G'(\theta)^4
\left\{
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{v=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+v}\varrho^{|r-v|}
\right\}^2.
\label{eq:dmop-backward-additive-fourth-moment}
\end{align}

It remains only to pass from a selected ancestral line to the empirical
conditional laws in Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-genealogical-lag-moments}).
Let the finer conditioning include the realized ancestor indices and let
the coarser conditioning include only the location arrays.  For the second
moment, the conditional variance identity gives
\begin{align}
&\E\left[
\Var\left\{
\left.\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-s}h_{s,i}(X_s)
\right|\hbox{ locations and ancestor indices}
\right\}
\,\middle|\,\hbox{ location arrays}
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\Var\left\{
\left.\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-s}h_{s,i}(X_s)
\right|\hbox{ location arrays}
\right\}.
\label{eq:dmop-lag-law-total-variance}
\end{align}
For the fourth moment, $(a+b)^4\leq8a^4+8b^4$ and conditional Jensen
give
\begin{align}
&\E\left[
\left|
S-\E(S\mid\hbox{ locations and ancestor indices})
\right|^4
\,\middle|\,\hbox{ location arrays}
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
16\E\left[
\left|
S-\E(S\mid\hbox{ location arrays})
\right|^4
\,\middle|\,\hbox{ location arrays}
\right],
\label{eq:dmop-lag-law-total-fourth-moment}
\end{align}
where $S$ in this display is the finite sum appearing in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-genealogical-lag-moments}).  Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-backward-additive-variance}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-backward-additive-fourth-moment}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-lag-law-total-variance}), and
(\ref{eq:dmop-lag-law-total-fourth-moment}) prove
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-genealogical-lag-moments}).

\medskip\noindent\emph{Step 6: continuation when $t<s$.}\quad
We now propagate a current function from its creation time.  For
$t\leq n$, define
\begin{equation}
\widetilde Q_{t,n}(f)
=
\begin{cases}
f,&t=n,\\
Q_{t,n-1}\{M_n(f)\},&t<n.
\end{cases}
\label{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup}
\end{equation}
The definition of $\Phi_{t,n}$ gives
\begin{equation}
\Phi_{t,n}(\mu)(f)
=
\frac{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,n}(f)}
{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-ratio}
\end{equation}
For $t<n$, $\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)=Q_{t,n-1}(1)$, so
Equation~(\ref{eq:weak-bias-uniform-normalizer-ratio}) gives the first
bound below.  For every $t\leq r<n$, the normalized one-step kernel
below satisfies
\begin{align}
&\frac{
M_{r+1}(x,du)\widetilde Q_{r+1,n}(1)(u)
}{
M_{r+1}\{\widetilde Q_{r+1,n}(1)\}(x)
}                                                           \notag\\
&\quad\geq
\frac{\underbar M}{\bar M}
\frac{
\widetilde Q_{r+1,n}(1)(u)\,du
}{
\int\widetilde Q_{r+1,n}(1)(v)\,dv
}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-one-step-minorization}
\end{align}
Its Dobrushin coefficient is therefore at most $\varrho$.  Composing
these kernels exactly as in Equations~
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-forward-kernel-factorization}) and
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-normalized-semigroup-contraction}) gives
\begin{align}
\sup_{x,x'}
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)(x)}
{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)(x')}
&\leq C,                                                     \notag\\
\operatorname{osc}\left\{
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(f)}
{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)}
\right\}
&\leq
\varrho^{n-t}\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-contraction}
\end{align}
The same display is immediate at $t=n$.

For the current predictor continuation in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-continuation}),
differentiating its normalized ratio gives
\begin{align}
D_\mu H_{t,n}(x)
&=
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)(x)}
{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)}
\left\{
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(h_{n,i})(x)}
{\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)(x)}
-H_{t,n}(\mu)
\right\}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-current-score-first-variation}
\end{align}
Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-contraction}) and
(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}) imply
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{osc}\{D_\mu H_{t,n}\}
\leq
CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-t}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-current-score-derivative-bound}
\end{equation}
Applying the single-ratio identity
(\ref{eq:auxiliary-exact-ratio-expansion}) to $H_{t,n}$ shows that
\begin{align}
&\left|
H_{t,n}(\nu)-H_{t,n}(\mu)
-(\nu-\mu)D_\mu H_{t,n}
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
C\left|(\nu-\mu)(a_H)\right|
\left|(\nu-\mu)D_\mu H_{t,n}\right|,
\label{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-remainder-bound}
\end{align}
where the normalized denominator
$a_H=\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)/\{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,n}(1)\}$
satisfies $\mu(a_H)=1$ and $C^{-1}\leq a_H\leq C$, so
$\nu(a_H)\geq C^{-1}$.  The
two empirical factors in
Equation~(\ref{eq:auxiliary-exact-ratio-expansion}) have oscillations at
most $C$ and $CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-t}$, respectively, by
Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-contraction}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-score-derivative-bound}).

For $t<s\leq n$, put $\mu_s=\Phi_{t,s}(\mu)$ and
\begin{equation}
d_{s,n}=D_{\mu_s}C_{s,n}^{(s)}.
\end{equation}
At time $s$, the functions $h_{s,i}$, $q_{s,n}^{0}$, and
$q_{s,n}^{1}$ depend on the current location.  Hence
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-derivative-formula}) shows that
$d_{s,n}$ is a current function satisfying
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{osc}(d_{s,n})
\leq
CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-s}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-created-score-derivative-bound}
\end{equation}
Differentiating
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-backward-continuation-definition}) and
using Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-ratio}) give
\begin{align}
D_\mu C_{t,n}^{(s)}(x)
&=
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)(x)}
{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)}
\left\{
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(d_{s,n})(x)}
{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)(x)}
-\mu_s(d_{s,n})
\right\}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-future-score-chain-rule}
\end{align}
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-contraction}) applied
from $t$ to $s$, followed by
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-created-score-derivative-bound}), gives
\begin{align}
\operatorname{osc}\left\{
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(d_{s,n})}
{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)}
\right\}
&\leq
\varrho^{s-t}\operatorname{osc}(d_{s,n})                  \notag\\
&\leq
CG'(\theta)\varrho^{s-t}\varrho^{n-s}                    \notag\\
&=
CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-t}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-future-score-two-contractions}
\end{align}
The normalizer bound in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-predictor-semigroup-contraction}) now
proves
\begin{align}
\operatorname{osc}\{D_\mu H_{t,n}\}
&\leq CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-t},                             \notag\\
\operatorname{osc}\{D_\mu C_{t,n}^{(s)}\}
&\leq CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-t},
\qquad t<s\leq n.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-future-current-derivative-bounds}
\end{align}

It remains to verify the quadratic remainder for $C_{t,n}^{(s)}$ when
$t<s$.  Its two normalized ratios are
\begin{equation}
\mathcal R_{l,t,s}(\lambda)
=
\frac{
\lambda\widetilde Q_{t,s}(h_{s,i}q_{s,n}^{l})
}{
\lambda\widetilde Q_{t,s}(q_{s,n}^{l})
},
\qquad l\in\{0,1\},
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-ratios}
\end{equation}
and
$C_{t,n}^{(s)}=\mathcal R_{1,t,s}-\mathcal R_{0,t,s}$.
The semigroup recursion gives
\begin{equation}
\widetilde Q_{t,s}(q_{s,n}^{l})=q_{t,n}^{l},
\qquad l\in\{0,1\}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-denominator-identity}
\end{equation}
Consequently, the normalized denominator factors are precisely the
$a_l$ in Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-ratios}), and
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{osc}(a_0)\leq C,\qquad
\operatorname{osc}(a_1-a_0)\leq C\varrho^{n-t},\qquad
\inf_l\nu(a_l)\geq C^{-1}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-A-factor-bounds}
\end{equation}
The first variation of the difference is
$D_\mu C_{t,n}^{(s)}$, already bounded in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-current-derivative-bounds}).  For
the first single ratio, the chain rule gives
\begin{align}
D_\mu\mathcal R_{1,t,s}(x)
&=
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)(x)}
{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)}
\left[
\frac{
\widetilde Q_{t,s}
(D_{\mu_s}\mathcal R_{1,s,s})(x)}
{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(1)(x)}
-\mu_s(D_{\mu_s}\mathcal R_{1,s,s})
\right].
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-single-ratio-chain-rule}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\begin{align}
\operatorname{osc}(D_\mu\mathcal R_{1,t,s})
&\leq
C\varrho^{s-t}
\operatorname{osc}(D_{\mu_s}\mathcal R_{1,s,s})            \notag\\
&\leq CG'(\theta).
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-single-ratio-bound}
\end{align}
The last inequality follows directly from
Equation~(\ref{eq:auxiliary-normalized-ratio-definition}), the bounded
normalizer ratios, and
$\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})\leq2G'(\theta)$.
Apply Claim~\ref{claim:normalized-ratio-perturbations} with
\begin{equation}
a_l=
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(q_{s,n}^{l})}
{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,s}(q_{s,n}^{l})},
\qquad
b_l=
\frac{\widetilde Q_{t,s}(h_{s,i}q_{s,n}^{l})}
{\mu\widetilde Q_{t,s}(q_{s,n}^{l})}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-ratio-factors}
\end{equation}
Equations~(\ref{eq:auxiliary-paired-ratio-bound}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-A-factor-bounds}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-current-derivative-bounds}), and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-single-ratio-bound}) give
\begin{align}
&\left|
C_{t,n}^{(s)}(\nu)-C_{t,n}^{(s)}(\mu)
-(\nu-\mu)D_\mu C_{t,n}^{(s)}
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
C^2\left|(\nu-\mu)(a_1-a_0)\right|
\left|(\nu-\mu)D_\mu\mathcal R_{1,t,s}\right|              \notag\\
&\qquad+
C\left|(\nu-\mu)(a_0)\right|
\left|(\nu-\mu)D_\mu C_{t,n}^{(s)}\right|.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-quadratic-remainder}
\end{align}
In each product on the right, one oscillation is bounded by
$C\varrho^{n-t}$ and the other by $CG'(\theta)$.
Together with Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-quadratic-remainder}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-remainder-bound}), this proves
the required first- and second-order continuation bounds for every
$t,s,n$.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Step 7: current and future coefficient sums.}\quad
After collecting all score contributions at their local sampling time
$t$, those with $t<s\leq n$ have total coefficient
\begin{align}
&CG'(\theta)
\sum_{s=t+1}^{N}\sum_{n=s}^{N}
\alpha^{n-s}\varrho^{s-t}\varrho^{n-s}                    \notag\\
&\quad\leq
CG'(\theta)
\left(\sum_{a=1}^{\infty}\varrho^a\right)
\left(\sum_{b=0}^{\infty}(\alpha\varrho)^b\right)          \notag\\
&\quad=
\frac{CG'(\theta)\varrho}
{(1-\varrho)(1-\alpha\varrho)}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-future-score-coefficient-sum}
\end{align}
The current predictor terms in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-telescope}) contribute
\begin{equation}
CG'(\theta)\sum_{n=t}^{N}\varrho^{n-t}
\leq\frac{CG'(\theta)}{1-\varrho}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-coefficient-sum}
\end{equation}

\iffalse
The same bound can be seen directly on the current
location--log-weight-gradient
pair.  For this calculation only, write
\begin{equation}
w_{t,i}
=
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_t^{F,\theta}\right|_{\theta=\phi}.
\end{equation}
Consider two pairs $(x,w_{t,i})$ and $(x',w'_{t,i})$ at time $t$.
For each terminal time $t+r$, couple the normalized forward location
kernels in Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-normalized-forward-product}).
Equation~(\ref{eq:weak-bias-forward-minorization}) permits the coupling
to be chosen so that, at every intermediate time $t+q$,
\begin{equation}
\Pr\{X_{t+q}\ne X'_{t+q}\}
\leq
\varrho^q.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-location-coupling}
\end{equation}
Iteration of Equation~(\ref{eq:location-weight-gradient-pair-recursion})
gives
\begin{align}
w_{t+r,i}
&=
\alpha^r w_{t,i}
+\sum_{q=1}^{r}\alpha^{r-q}h_{t+q,i}(X_{t+q}),             \notag\\
w'_{t+r,i}
&=
\alpha^r w'_{t,i}
+\sum_{q=1}^{r}\alpha^{r-q}h_{t+q,i}(X'_{t+q}).
\label{eq:dmop-direct-coupled-weight-gradient-iteration}
\end{align}
Equations~(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-location-coupling}), and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-coupled-weight-gradient-iteration}) imply
\begin{align}
\E|w_{t+r,i}-w'_{t+r,i}|
&\leq
\alpha^r|w_{t,i}-w'_{t,i}|
+2G'(\theta)\sum_{q=1}^{r}\alpha^{r-q}\varrho^q.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-coupled-weight-gradient-bound}
\end{align}
The remaining convolution is the finite sum
\begin{equation}
\sum_{q=1}^{r}\alpha^{r-q}\varrho^q
=
\begin{cases}
\displaystyle
\frac{\varrho(\alpha^r-\varrho^r)}{\alpha-\varrho},
&\alpha\ne\varrho,\\[2mm]
r\alpha^r,&\alpha=\varrho.
\end{cases}
\label{eq:dmop-direct-pair-forgetting-convolution}
\end{equation}
Hence Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-coupled-weight-gradient-bound})
shows that the coupled location--log-weight-gradient pair forgets its
initial condition at the
geometric rate $\max(\alpha,\varrho)^r$ apart from the factor $r$ when
$\alpha=\varrho$.  This does not assert total-variation contraction of
the pair: deterministic differences in the second coordinate can remain
mutually singular even while their absolute distance contracts.
Let $L=N-t$.  The finite identities
\begin{align}
(1-\alpha)\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\alpha^r+\alpha^L&=1,
\label{eq:dmop-direct-initial-weight-coefficient-one}
\\
(1-\alpha)\sum_{r=q}^{L-1}\alpha^{r-q}
+\alpha^{L-q}&=1,
\qquad 1\leq q\leq L,
\label{eq:dmop-direct-future-input-coefficient-one}
\end{align}
are exactly the coefficients in the full-score summation-by-parts identity
(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}).  Multiply Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-coupled-weight-gradient-bound}) by $1-\alpha$ for
$r<L$, retain coefficient one for $r=L$, and sum.  Interchanging the two
finite sums and using Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-initial-weight-coefficient-one})--
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-input-coefficient-one}) gives
\begin{align}
&(1-\alpha)\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}
\E|w_{t+r,i}-w'_{t+r,i}|
+\E|w_{t+L,i}-w'_{t+L,i}|                                \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\left|w_{t,i}-w'_{t,i}\right|
+2G'(\theta)\sum_{q=1}^{L}\varrho^q                      \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{2G'(\theta)}{1-\alpha}
+\frac{2G'(\theta)\varrho}{1-\varrho}                   \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{1-\alpha},
\label{eq:dmop-direct-pair-full-score-continuation-bound}
\end{align}
where Equation~(\ref{eq:unrolled-filtered-weight}) gives
$|w_{t,i}-w'_{t,i}|\leq2G'(\theta)/(1-\alpha)$.
Thus the pair need not be total-variation mixing: location coupling and
the affine contraction of its log-weight-gradient component give precisely
the score-continuation bound used in Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-aggregated-local-oscillation}).
\fi

\medskip\noindent\emph{Step 8: local particle errors.}\quad
We next define the two local terms rather than citing a fixed-terminal
formula for their sum.  For the signed empirical error
$m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J$, put
\begin{align}
\Gamma_{t,i}^J
&=\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}
\left[
\sum_{n=t}^{N}D_{\mu_t^J}H_{t,n}
+\sum_{n=t}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{n}
\alpha^{n-s}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{(s)}
\right],
\label{eq:dmop-direct-centered-local-field-definition}
\\
B_{t,i}^J
&=
\sum_{n=t}^{N}
\left[
 H_{t,n}\{m(\xi_t^P)\}-H_{t,n}(\mu_t^J)
-\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}D_{\mu_t^J}H_{t,n}
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\quad+
\sum_{n=t}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\alpha^{n-s}
\left[
C_{t,n}^{(s)}\{m(\xi_t^P)\}-C_{t,n}^{(s)}(\mu_t^J)
-\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{(s)}
\right].
\label{eq:dmop-direct-local-remainder-definition}
\end{align}
Substitute the telescopes
Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-telescope}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-continuation-full-telescope}) into the difference
of Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-exact-predictor-decomposition}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-exact-population-decomposition}), and then use
Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-centered-local-field-definition}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-remainder-definition}).  Term-by-term
collection gives the exact identity
\begin{equation}
Z_i^J-z_i^\alpha
=
\sum_{n=1}^{N}R_{n,i}^J
+\sum_{t=1}^{N}\Gamma_{t,i}^J
+\sum_{t=1}^{N}B_{t,i}^J,
\label{eq:dmop-direct-local-error-decomposition}
\end{equation}
which uses the same local perturbation construction as Section~5, in
particular Equation~(5.5), and Appendix~A.1 of \citet{delMoral11}, with the DMOP
sums taken only after the local fields have been formed.  Conditionally
on the information just before the combined time-$t$ multinomial-selection
and mutation block, $m(\xi_t^P)$ is the empirical measure of $J$
independent draws with common law $\mu_t^J$.
We now use the lag moments before summing over the historical index.
Within the next calculation only, abbreviate
\begin{equation}
S=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-s}h_{s,i}.
\label{eq:dmop-local-discounted-history}
\end{equation}
Because the functional in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-continuation-functional}) is linear in its
displayed $h_{s,i}$, for every $n\geq t$,
\begin{equation}
\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{n-s}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{(s)}
=
\alpha^{n-t}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{S},
\label{eq:dmop-local-past-score-linear-combination}
\end{equation}
where the superscript $S$ means only that $S$ replaces $h_{s,i}$ in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-continuation-functional}).

Write $\mu=\mu_t^J$ in this paragraph.  With the $a_l$ from
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-ratios}), put
$\mathcal R_l^S(\mu)=\mu(a_lS)$.  Since $\mu(a_l)=1$ and the $a_l$ are
uniformly bounded,
\begin{align}
|\mathcal R_l^S(\mu)-\mu(S)|
&=
|\mu\{(a_l-1)(S-\mu S)\}|                                \notag\\
&\leq
C\mu\{(S-\mu S)^2\}^{1/2},
\label{eq:dmop-local-weighted-mean-bound}
\end{align}
while Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-derivative-formula}) and
$\|a_1-a_0\|_\infty\leq C\varrho^{n-t}$ give
\begin{align}
|\mathcal R_1^S(\mu)-\mathcal R_0^S(\mu)|
&=
|\mu\{(a_1-a_0)(S-\mu S)\}|                              \notag\\
&\leq
C\varrho^{n-t}\mu\{(S-\mu S)^2\}^{1/2},
\label{eq:dmop-local-two-weighted-means}
\end{align}
Here $\mu(a_1-a_0)=0$ by $\mu(a_0)=\mu(a_1)=1$, so the asserted
supremum bound follows from
$\operatorname{osc}(a_1-a_0)\leq C\varrho^{n-t}$ in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-derivative-oscillation}), and hence
\begin{align}
\mu\{(D_\mu C_{t,n}^{S})^2\}^{1/2}
&\leq
C\varrho^{n-t}\mu\{(S-\mu S)^2\}^{1/2},
\label{eq:dmop-local-past-field-l2}
\end{align}
\begin{align}
\mu\{|D_\mu C_{t,n}^{S}|^4\}^{1/4}
&\leq
C\varrho^{n-t}\mu\{|S-\mu S|^4\}^{1/4}.
\label{eq:dmop-local-past-field-l4}
\end{align}
The fourth-moment inequality follows from the same two displayed
identities and Lyapunov's inequality,
\begin{equation}
\{\mu((S-\mu S)^2)\}^{1/2}
\leq
\{\mu(|S-\mu S|^4)\}^{1/4}.
\end{equation}

Minkowski's inequality, Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-local-past-score-linear-combination}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-local-past-field-l2}), and the geometric sum give
\begin{align}
&\mu_t^J\left[
\left\{
\sum_{n=t}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{t}
\alpha^{n-s}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{(s)}
\right\}^2
\right]^{1/2}                                              \notag\\
&\quad\leq
C\mu_t^J\{(S-\mu_t^JS)^2\}^{1/2}
\sum_{n=t}^{N}(\alpha\varrho)^{n-t}                      \notag\\
&\quad\leq
C\mu_t^J\{(S-\mu_t^JS)^2\}^{1/2}.
\label{eq:dmop-local-past-field-summed-l2}
\end{align}
All remaining first-order terms are current functions at time $t$.
Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-score-derivative-bound}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-current-derivative-bounds}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-score-coefficient-sum}), and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-coefficient-sum}) give their
combined $L^2(\mu_t^J)$ norm at most $CG'(\theta)$.  Every first
variation is centered under $\mu_t^J$.  Therefore Claim~
\ref{claim:conditional-empirical-moments} gives
\begin{align}
\E(\Gamma_{t,i}^J\mid\mathcal F_{t-1})&=0,                  \notag\\
\E(\Gamma_{t,i}^J)^2
&=
\frac1J\E\,\mu_t^J\left[
\left\{
\sum_{n=t}^{N}D_{\mu_t^J}H_{t,n}
+\sum_{n=t}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{n}
\alpha^{n-s}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{(s)}
\right\}^2
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-local-field-centered-bounds}
\end{align}
Here $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ is the sigma-field just before that combined
selection--mutation block: $m(\xi_{t-1}^P)$ is measurable, but the parent
selections and mutations used to construct $m(\xi_t^P)$ have not yet been
revealed.  The last inequality is
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-genealogical-lag-moments}) with $q=2$; its
right side also dominates the squared $CG'(\theta)$ current-function
bound because its $r=u=0$ summand equals one.

The same reordering sharpens the quadratic remainder.  For each
$n\geq t$, first sum the terms with $s\leq t$ inside the two products in
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-quadratic-remainder}).
By linearity, the resulting products are the same products with
$\alpha^{n-t}S$ in place of $h_{s,i}$.  Thus each product has an
outside factor $\alpha^{n-t}$.  Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-local-weighted-mean-bound})--
(\ref{eq:dmop-local-past-field-l4}) give the conditional fourth-moment
bound
\begin{equation}
C\varrho^{4(n-t)}
\mu_t^J\{|S-\mu_t^JS|^4\}.
\end{equation}
To write out the empirical-product step, condition on
$\mathcal F_{t-1}$ and apply Cauchy--Schwarz.  The exact fourth-moment
identity in Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-empirical-fourth-moment})
gives, for each product on the right of
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-quadratic-remainder}),
\begin{align}
&\E\left[
\left.
\left|
\Big[\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}(a_1-a_0)\Big]
\Big[\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}
  D_{\mu_t^J}\mathcal R_1^S\Big]
\right|^2
\right|\mathcal F_{t-1}
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\E\left[
\left.\Big[\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}(a_1-a_0)\Big]^4
\right|\mathcal F_{t-1}\right]^{1/2}
\E\left[
\left.\Big[\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}
D_{\mu_t^J}\mathcal R_1^S\Big]^4
\right|\mathcal F_{t-1}\right]^{1/2}                       \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{C\varrho^{2(n-t)}}{J^2}
\left[\mu_t^J\{|S-\mu_t^JS|^4\}\right]^{1/2}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-empirical-product-fourth-moment}
\end{align}
The same calculation, with $a_0$ and
$D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{S}$, gives the same right side.  Taking the square
root of Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-empirical-product-fourth-moment}), then applying
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-genealogical-lag-moments}) with $q=4$, gives
\begin{align}
&\left\|
\Big[\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}(a_1-a_0)\Big]
\Big[\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}D_{\mu_t^J}\mathcal R_1^S\Big]
\right\|_{L^2}                                              \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)\varrho^{n-t}}J
\left\{
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right\}^{1/2}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-empirical-product-lag-bound}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\begin{align}
&\left\|
\sum_{n=t}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{n-s}
\left[
C_{t,n}^{(s)}\{m(\xi_t^P)\}-C_{t,n}^{(s)}(\mu_t^J)
-\{m(\xi_t^P)-\mu_t^J\}D_{\mu_t^J}C_{t,n}^{(s)}
\right]
\right\|_{L^2}                                             \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}J
\left\{
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right\}^{1/2}
\sum_{n=t}^{N}(\alpha\varrho)^{n-t}                       \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}J
\left\{
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right\}^{1/2}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-past-remainder-lag-bound}
\end{align}
For the $H_{t,n}$ terms and the terms with $t<s$, both empirical
factors are current functions.  Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-remainder-bound}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-propagated-quadratic-remainder}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-score-coefficient-sum}), and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-current-predictor-coefficient-sum}) bound their
combined unconditional $L^2$ norm by $CG'(\theta)/J$.  Since the
$r=u=0$ summand again equals one, combining this bound with
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-past-remainder-lag-bound}) gives
\begin{align}
\|B_{t,i}^J\|_{L^2}
&\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}J
\left\{
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right\}^{1/2}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-local-field-remainder-bound}
\end{align}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Step 9: martingale and remainder summation.}\quad
Expose the terminal multinomial selections in chronological order.  Before
the time-$n$ selection, the prediction cloud $\xi_{n,1:J}^P$ and every
$R_{m,i}^J$ with $m<n$ are measurable, while Equation~
(\ref{eq:aggregated-terminal-resampling-error}) gives conditional mean zero
for $R_{n,i}^J$.  Hence
\begin{equation}
\E(R_{m,i}^JR_{n,i}^J)=0,
\qquad m<n.
\label{eq:terminal-resampling-martingale-orthogonality}
\end{equation}
Likewise, expose the prediction draws in chronological order.  The
sigma-field $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ in Step 8 contains every
$\Gamma_{r,i}^J$ with $r<t$, and
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-field-centered-bounds}) gives
\begin{equation}
\E(\Gamma_{r,i}^J\Gamma_{t,i}^J)=0,
\qquad r<t.
\label{eq:local-field-martingale-orthogonality}
\end{equation}
The two families need not be mutually orthogonal because the selection
at time $n$ also enters the next prediction cloud.  Applying
$\|A+B\|_{L^2}^2\leq2\|A\|_{L^2}^2+2\|B\|_{L^2}^2$ first, and then
martingale orthogonality separately within each family, gives
\begin{align}
\left\|
\sum_{n=1}^{N}R_{n,i}^J
+\sum_{t=1}^{N}\Gamma_{t,i}^J
\right\|_{L^2}^2
&\leq
2\sum_{n=1}^{N}\|R_{n,i}^J\|_{L^2}^2
+2\sum_{t=1}^{N}\|\Gamma_{t,i}^J\|_{L^2}^2               \notag\\
&\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-centered-local-sum}
\end{align}
For the quadratic terms, Minkowski's inequality gives
\begin{align}
\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{N}B_{t,i}^J\right\|_{L^2}
&\leq
\sum_{t=1}^{N}\|B_{t,i}^J\|_{L^2}                         \notag\\
&\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}J
\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\left\{
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right\}^{1/2}.
\label{eq:dmop-direct-quadratic-sum}
\end{align}
Combining Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-error-decomposition}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-centered-local-sum}), and
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-quadratic-sum}), and using
$(a+b)^2\leq2a^2+2b^2$, yields
\begin{equation}
\E|Z_i^J-z_i^\alpha|^2
\leq
CG'(\theta)^2
\left[
\frac1J\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
+\frac1{J^2}
\left\{
\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\left(
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right)^{1/2}
\right\}^2
\right].
\label{eq:dmop-direct-coordinate-particle-error}
\end{equation}
Since $\varrho^{|r-u|}\leq1$ and
$t\leq N$,
\begin{align}
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
&\leq
\left(\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r\right)^2,                 \notag\\
\left\{
\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\left(
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right)^{1/2}
\right\}^2
&\leq
N^2\left(\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r\right)^2.
\label{eq:dmop-lag-sums-coarse-bounds}
\end{align}
Summing over $i=1,\ldots,p$ proves
Equation~(\ref{eq:refined-dmop-alpha-direct-particle-error}).  Since
$z_i^\alpha$ is deterministic,
\begin{equation}
\Var(Z_i^J)
=\inf_{a\in\mathbb R}\E|Z_i^J-a|^2
\leq\E|Z_i^J-z_i^\alpha|^2.
\end{equation}
Summing this inequality over the coordinates proves the variance part of
Equation~(\ref{eq:refined-dmop-alpha-direct-particle-error}).

It remains to evaluate the lag sums.  For $\alpha<1$ and every $t$,
\begin{align}
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
&\leq
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}                               \notag\\
&=
\frac1{1-\alpha^2}
\left\{1+2\sum_{d=1}^{\infty}(\alpha\varrho)^d\right\}
                                                               \notag\\
&=
\frac{1+\alpha\varrho}
{(1-\alpha^2)(1-\alpha\varrho)}.
\label{eq:dmop-infinite-lag-double-sum}
\end{align}
Substitution into Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-coordinate-particle-error}), followed by summation
over the coordinates, proves Equation~
(\ref{eq:refined-dmop-alpha-lag-particle-error}).  At $\alpha=1$,
\begin{align}
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}\varrho^{|r-u|}
&=
t+2\sum_{d=1}^{t-1}(t-d)\varrho^d                       \notag\\
&\leq
t\frac{1+\varrho}{1-\varrho},                            \notag\\
\sum_{t=1}^{N}t&\leq N^2,                                \notag\\
\left(\sum_{t=1}^{N}\sqrt t\right)^2&\leq N^3.
\label{eq:dmop-one-finite-lag-sums}
\end{align}
Equations~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-coordinate-particle-error}) and
(\ref{eq:dmop-one-finite-lag-sums}) give the second member of the
minimum in Equation~(\ref{eq:refined-dmop-one-lag-particle-error}).
For the first member, Equations~
(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}) and
(\ref{eq:population-full-score-summation-by-parts}), together with
Equation~(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}), give, for every
coordinate $i$,
\begin{align}
&(1-\alpha)\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-k}
+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}                                \notag\\
&\qquad=
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)
+\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r
=N,
\label{eq:dmop-full-score-coefficient-identity}
\\
\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^1(\theta)\right|
&\leq NG'(\theta),                                         \notag\\
\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\ell^1(\theta)\right|
&\leq NG'(\theta),                                         \notag\\
\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^1(\theta)
-\partial_{\theta_i}\ell^1(\theta)\right|^2
&\leq4N^2G'(\theta)^2,                                    \notag\\
\Var\{\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^1(\theta)\}
&\leq N^2G'(\theta)^2.
\label{eq:dmop-one-pathwise-particle-error}
\end{align}
Summing Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-one-pathwise-particle-error}) over
$i=1,\ldots,p$ and combining the two valid bounds proves
Equation~(\ref{eq:refined-dmop-one-lag-particle-error}).
\end{proof}

\begin{cor}[Particle bias of the DMOP-$\alpha$ score]
\label{lem:dmop-full-particle-bias}
Under the conditions of Lemma~\ref{lem:dmop-alpha-variance}, for
$\alpha\in[0,1)$,
\begin{align}
\left\|
\E\nabla_\theta\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
-\nabla_\theta\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\|_2
&\leq
\frac{C\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)}{J(1-\alpha)},
\label{eq:conditional-score-particle-bias-direct}
\\
\left\|
\E\nabla_\theta\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)
-\nabla_\theta\ell^\alpha(\theta)
\right\|_2
&\leq
\frac{C\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)}J
\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\left(
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right)^{1/2}                                                \notag\\
&\leq
\frac{CN\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)}J
\left\{
\frac{1+\alpha\varrho}
{(1-\alpha^2)(1-\alpha\varrho)}
\right\}^{1/2}.
\label{eq:full-score-particle-bias-direct}
\end{align}
\end{cor}

\begin{proof}[Proof.]
Fix a coordinate $i$ and a conditional-score time $n$.  Restrict the exact
local decomposition in Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-error-decomposition}) to this value of $n$.
The terminal resampling term and every first-order local field have
conditional mean zero.  The quadratic ratio remainders are the only
terms with nonzero expectation.  Equations~
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-quadratic-remainder}),
(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-future-current-derivative-bounds}), and
(\ref{eq:conditional-empirical-product-moment}) therefore give
\begin{align}
&\left|
\E\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
-\partial_{\theta_i}\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}J
\left\{
\sum_{t=1}^{n}\varrho^{n-t}
+\sum_{t=1}^{n}\sum_{s=1}^{t}
\alpha^{n-s}\varrho^{n-t}
+\sum_{t=1}^{n-1}\sum_{s=t+1}^{n}
\alpha^{n-s}\varrho^{s-t}\varrho^{n-s}
\right\}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-bias-three-sums}
\end{align}
The three sums in Equation~
(\ref{eq:conditional-score-bias-three-sums}) satisfy
\begin{align}
\sum_{t=1}^{n}\varrho^{n-t}
&\leq \frac1{1-\varrho},                                   \notag\\
\sum_{t=1}^{n}\sum_{s=1}^{t}
\alpha^{n-s}\varrho^{n-t}
&\leq
\left\{\sum_{a=0}^{\infty}(\alpha\varrho)^a\right\}
\left\{\sum_{b=0}^{\infty}\alpha^b\right\}
=\frac1{(1-\alpha\varrho)(1-\alpha)},                    \notag\\
\sum_{t=1}^{n-1}\sum_{s=t+1}^{n}
\alpha^{n-s}\varrho^{s-t}\varrho^{n-s}
&\leq
\left\{\sum_{a=1}^{\infty}\varrho^a\right\}
\left\{\sum_{b=0}^{\infty}(\alpha\varrho)^b\right\}
=\frac{\varrho}{(1-\varrho)(1-\alpha\varrho)}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-bias-three-sum-bounds}
\end{align}
Since $1-\alpha\varrho\geq1-\varrho$, substitution into
Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-bias-three-sums}) gives
\begin{equation}
\left|
\E\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
-\partial_{\theta_i}\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right|
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{J(1-\alpha)}.
\end{equation}
Taking the Euclidean norm over the coordinates proves
Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-particle-bias-direct}).  For the full
score, take expectations in the exact decomposition
Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-error-decomposition}).  Equations~
(\ref{eq:terminal-resampling-martingale-orthogonality}) and
(\ref{eq:local-field-martingale-orthogonality}), together with the
conditional mean-zero statements that precede them, give
\begin{align}
\E R_{n,i}^J&=0,
&
\E\Gamma_{t,i}^J&=0.
\end{align}
Therefore Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-direct-local-field-remainder-bound})
and the triangle inequality give, coordinate by coordinate,
\begin{align}
\left|\E Z_i^J-z_i^\alpha\right|
&=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{N}\E B_{t,i}^J\right|                 \notag\\
&\leq\sum_{t=1}^{N}\|B_{t,i}^J\|_{L^2}                   \notag\\
&\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}J
\sum_{t=1}^{N}
\left(
\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}
\alpha^{r+u}\varrho^{|r-u|}
\right)^{1/2}.
\end{align}
Taking the Euclidean norm over $i=1,\ldots,p$ gives the first inequality
in Equation~(\ref{eq:full-score-particle-bias-direct}).  For
$\alpha<1$, Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-infinite-lag-double-sum}) bounds each
double sum by
$(1+\alpha\varrho)/\{(1-\alpha^2)(1-\alpha\varrho)\}$, which proves the
second inequality.
\end{proof}

\iffalse
\begin{proof}[Superseded proof.]

Equation~(\ref{eq:unrolled-filtered-weight}) and
Equation~(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}) give, for every
coordinate $i$,
\begin{align}
\left|
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n,j}^{F,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
\right|
&\leq
\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}\alpha^r
\left|
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log
g_{n-r,j}^{A,F,\theta}\right|_{\theta=\phi}
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\leq
G'(\theta)\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}\alpha^r,
\label{eq:filtered-weight-gradient-bound}
\\
\left|
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n,j}^{P,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
\right|
&=
\alpha
\left|
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n-1,j}^{F,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\leq
G'(\theta)\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}\alpha^r.
\label{eq:prediction-weight-gradient-bound}
\end{align}
We next record the particle bound for a bounded current-particle
function and write out the filtering step that it does not cover
directly.  The single-step measurement gradient $h_{s,i}$ is a function
of the time-$s$ particle at the time it is created.  By contrast, the
accumulated gradients in Equations~
(\ref{eq:filtered-weight-gradient-bound}) and
(\ref{eq:prediction-weight-gradient-bound}) are functions of ancestral
trajectories.  They need not have the mixing properties of the current
particle cloud.  Accordingly, the current-particle bound below is used
only for $h_{s,i}$ at time $s$; its propagation to a later time is
handled by the explicit telescope in Equation~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-continuation-telescope}).
Lemma~2.3 of \citet{karjalainen23}, with $p=4$, states that for every
bounded real function $f$ of a prediction particle,
\begin{equation}
\left\|\eta_n^J(f)-\eta_n(f)\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{c(4)}{\sqrt J}\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:karjalainen-predictor-l4}
\end{equation}
Below this is applied only to a single-step score at the time at which
that score is created.  It is not applied to an accumulated weight
gradient or to an individual historical coordinate at a later time.

For completeness, consider the corresponding filtered average
\begin{equation}
Y=\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^Jf(x_{n,j}^F).
\end{equation}
Conditionally on $\eta_n^J$, multinomial resampling makes the
$x_{n,j}^F$ independent with common law
$\pi_n^J=\Psi_n(\eta_n^J)$. If a filtered gradient coordinate is obtained
by a deterministic post-selection update, $f$ denotes its composition
with that update as a function of the selected prediction particle. Put
\begin{equation}
Z_j=f(x_{n,j}^F)-\pi_n^J(f).
\end{equation}
Then, conditionally on $\eta_n^J$, the $Z_j$ are independent,
$\E(Z_j\mid\eta_n^J)=0$, and
$|Z_j|\leq\operatorname{osc}(f)$. Expanding the fourth power and using
conditional independence gives
\begin{align}
&\E\left[
\left.
\left|Y-\pi_n^J(f)\right|^4
\right|\eta_n^J
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\quad=
\frac1{J^4}
\left\{
\sum_{j=1}^J\E(Z_j^4\mid\eta_n^J)
+6\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq J}
\E(Z_j^2\mid\eta_n^J)
\E(Z_k^2\mid\eta_n^J)
\right\}                                                    \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{J+3J(J-1)}{J^4}\operatorname{osc}(f)^4               \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{3}{J^2}\operatorname{osc}(f)^4.
\label{eq:conditional-filter-resampling-fourth-moment}
\end{align}
Taking expectations and fourth roots yields
\begin{equation}
\left\|Y-\pi_n^J(f)\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{3^{1/4}}{\sqrt J}\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:conditional-filter-resampling-l4}
\end{equation}

It remains to compare the random weighted prediction measure with the
deterministic filter.  Since $\pi_n=\Psi_n(\eta_n)$,
\begin{align}
\pi_n^J(f)-\pi_n(f)
&=
\frac{\eta_n^J(G_nf)}{\eta_n^J(G_n)}-\pi_n(f)              \notag\\
&=
\frac{
\eta_n^J\left[G_n\{f-\pi_n(f)\}\right]
}{
\eta_n^J(G_n)
}                                                           \notag\\
&=
\frac{
(\eta_n^J-\eta_n)\left[G_n\{f-\pi_n(f)\}\right]
}{
\eta_n^J(G_n)
},
\label{eq:filter-bayes-ratio-centered}
\end{align}
where the last equality uses
\begin{equation}
\eta_n\left[G_n\{f-\pi_n(f)\}\right]
=
\eta_n(G_nf)-\pi_n(f)\eta_n(G_n)
=0.
\end{equation}
Assumption~{\refAssumpBoundedMeasurement} gives
\begin{align}
\eta_n^J(G_n)
&\geq\underbar G,                                           \notag\\
\operatorname{osc}\left[G_n\{f-\pi_n(f)\}\right]
&\leq
2\bar G\operatorname{osc}(f).
\end{align}
Applying Equation~(\ref{eq:karjalainen-predictor-l4}) to the
centered function in Equation~(\ref{eq:filter-bayes-ratio-centered})
therefore gives
\begin{equation}
\left\|\pi_n^J(f)-\pi_n(f)\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{2c(4)\bar G}{\underbar G\sqrt J}
\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:filter-bayes-ratio-l4}
\end{equation}
Minkowski's inequality and Equations~
(\ref{eq:conditional-filter-resampling-l4})--
(\ref{eq:filter-bayes-ratio-l4}) now give the filtered analogue of
Karjalainen et al.'s predictor bound:
\begin{equation}
\left\|Y-\pi_n(f)\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{C}{\sqrt J}\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:filtered-particle-l4}
\end{equation}

For either the prediction or filtered average, let $y$ denote the
corresponding deterministic target. Jensen's inequality and the triangle
inequality give the required centered form:
\begin{align}
\|Y-\E Y\|_{L^4}
&\leq
\|Y-y\|_{L^4}+|\E(Y-y)|                                  \notag\\
&\leq
2\|Y-y\|_{L^4}.
\label{eq:l4-centering-bridge}
\end{align}

We next prove the corresponding bound for the exact consecutive-cloud
difference in Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-path-decomposition}).
Fix $1\leq r<n$, put $s=n-r$, and fix coordinate $i$.  Write
\begin{equation}
m(\xi_t^P)=\frac1J\sum_{a=1}^J\delta_{\xi_{t,a}^P}
\label{eq:prediction-path-empirical-measure}
\end{equation}
for the empirical measure of the prediction paths.  Propagation from
time $n-1$ to time $n$ preserves the time-$s$ contribution and its
particle index.  Hence
\begin{align}
h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
&=h_{s,i}(\xi_{n-1,a}),                                    \notag\\
m(\xi_n^P)(h_{s,i})
&=m(\xi_{n-1})(h_{s,i}).
\label{eq:historical-score-propagation-identity}
\end{align}
Let $k_1,\ldots,k_J$ be the time-$n$ multinomial resampling indices and
put
\begin{equation}
p_{n,a}=\frac{G_n(\xi_{n,a}^P)}{\sum_{b=1}^JG_n(\xi_{n,b}^P)}.
\label{eq:time-n-resampling-probability}
\end{equation}
Equations~(\ref{eq:lag-coefficient-definitions}) and
(\ref{eq:historical-score-propagation-identity}) give the pathwise
decomposition
\begin{align}
\Delta_{n,r,i}^J
&=
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^Jh_{s,i}(\xi_{n,k_j}^P)
-\frac1J\sum_{a=1}^Jh_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)                  \notag\\
&=
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^J
\left\{
h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,k_j}^P)
-\sum_{a=1}^Jp_{n,a}h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
\right\}                                                   \notag\\
&\quad+
\left\{
\sum_{a=1}^Jp_{n,a}h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
-\frac1J\sum_{a=1}^Jh_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
\right\}.
\label{eq:one-lag-resampling-predictable-decomposition}
\end{align}
Conditionally on the prediction paths, the summands in the first term
of Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-resampling-predictable-decomposition}) are
independent, centered, and bounded in absolute value by
$\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})$.  The same fourth-power expansion as in
Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-filter-resampling-fourth-moment}) gives
\begin{align}
&\E\left[
\left.
\left|
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^J
\left\{
h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,k_j}^P)
-\sum_{a=1}^Jp_{n,a}h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
\right\}
\right|^4
\right|\xi_{n,1:J}^P
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&\qquad\leq
\frac{3}{J^2}\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})^4.
\label{eq:one-lag-resampling-fourth-moment}
\end{align}
Equation~(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}) therefore implies
\begin{equation}
\left\|
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^J
\left\{
h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,k_j}^P)
-\sum_{a=1}^Jp_{n,a}h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
\right\}
\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{2\,3^{1/4}G'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}.
\label{eq:one-lag-resampling-l4}
\end{equation}

It remains to control the second, predictable term in Equation~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-resampling-predictable-decomposition}).  We write out
the backward telescope rather than treating $h_{s,i}$ as a current
function at time $n$.  Use the semigroup $Q_{t,u}$ from Equation~
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-semigroup-definition}) and, only for the following
calculation, put
\begin{align}
q_{t,n}^{1}
&=Q_{t,n}(1),                                                \notag\\
q_{t,n}^{0}
&=
\begin{cases}
Q_{t,n-1}(1),&s\leq t<n,\\
1,&t=n,
\end{cases}                                                 \notag\\
F_{t,n}
&=\frac{q_{t,n}^{1}}{q_{t,n}^{0}}.
\label{eq:one-lag-future-functions}
\end{align}
Thus $q_{n,n}^{1}=G_n$, $q_{n,n}^{0}=1$, and, for $s\leq t<n$,
\begin{equation}
q_{t,n}^{l}=G_tM_{t+1}(q_{t+1,n}^{l}),
\qquad l\in\{0,1\}.
\label{eq:one-lag-future-function-recursion}
\end{equation}
For a probability measure $\mu$ on prediction paths through time $t$,
put
\begin{equation}
C_{t,n}(\mu)
=
\frac{\mu(h_{s,i}q_{t,n}^{1})}{\mu(q_{t,n}^{1})}
-
\frac{\mu(h_{s,i}q_{t,n}^{0})}{\mu(q_{t,n}^{0})}.
\label{eq:one-lag-continuation-functional}
\end{equation}
At $t=n$, Equations~(\ref{eq:time-n-resampling-probability}) and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-future-functions}) give
\begin{align}
C_{n,n}\{m(\xi_n^P)\}
&=
\sum_{a=1}^Jp_{n,a}h_{s,i}(\xi_{n,a}^P)
-m(\xi_n^P)(h_{s,i}),                                     \notag\\
C_{n,n}(\eta_n)
&=
\rho_n(h_{s,i})-\rho_{n-1}(h_{s,i})
=\Delta_{n,r,i}.
\label{eq:one-lag-continuation-endpoints}
\end{align}

Let $\Phi_t$ be the prediction-measure update already used in Equation~
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-local-conditional-law}).  Since $h_{s,i}$ is
preserved by every mutation after time $s$, Equation~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-future-function-recursion}) gives, for $s<t\leq n$,
\begin{align}
\frac{\Phi_t(\mu)(h_{s,i}q_{t,n}^{l})}
{\Phi_t(\mu)(q_{t,n}^{l})}
&=
\frac{
\mu\{G_{t-1}M_t(h_{s,i}q_{t,n}^{l})\}
}{
\mu\{G_{t-1}M_t(q_{t,n}^{l})\}
}                                                          \notag\\
&=
\frac{
\mu\{h_{s,i}G_{t-1}M_t(q_{t,n}^{l})\}
}{
\mu\{G_{t-1}M_t(q_{t,n}^{l})\}
}                                                          \notag\\
&=
\frac{\mu(h_{s,i}q_{t-1,n}^{l})}
{\mu(q_{t-1,n}^{l})},
\qquad l\in\{0,1\}.
\label{eq:one-lag-continuation-consistency-one-ratio}
\end{align}
Subtracting the two cases $l=1$ and $l=0$ proves
\begin{equation}
C_{t,n}\{\Phi_t(\mu)\}=C_{t-1,n}(\mu).
\label{eq:one-lag-continuation-consistency}
\end{equation}
Consequently, the following telescope is an exact identity:
\begin{align}
&C_{n,n}\{m(\xi_n^P)\}-C_{n,n}(\eta_n)                    \notag\\
&\quad=
C_{s,n}\{m(\xi_s^P)\}-C_{s,n}(\eta_s)                    \notag\\
&\qquad+
\sum_{t=s+1}^{n}
\left[
C_{t,n}\{m(\xi_t^P)\}
-C_{t,n}\left\{\Phi_t(m(\xi_{t-1}^P))\right\}
\right].
\label{eq:one-lag-continuation-telescope}
\end{align}
Indeed, Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-continuation-consistency}) makes each
second term in the summand equal to
$C_{t-1,n}\{m(\xi_{t-1}^P)\}$, so all intermediate terms cancel; the
same consistency identity applied to the deterministic prediction
measures gives $C_{n,n}(\eta_n)=C_{s,n}(\eta_s)$.

We next bound every term in Equation~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-continuation-telescope}).  Equations~
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-uniform-normalizer-ratio}) and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-future-functions}) give a constant $R<\infty$ such
that
\begin{equation}
\sup_{x,x'}
\frac{q_{t,n}^{0}(x)}{q_{t,n}^{0}(x')}
\leq R,
\qquad s\leq t\leq n.
\label{eq:one-lag-q-zero-ratio}
\end{equation}
Moreover, $F_{t,n}$ is the conditional expectation of $G_n$ under the
normalized Feynman--Kac transitions from $t$ to $n$.  Therefore
\begin{equation}
\underbar G\leq F_{t,n}\leq\bar G.
\label{eq:one-lag-F-bounds}
\end{equation}
More explicitly, for $t<n$, canceling the common factor $G_t$ in
$q_{t,n}^{1}/q_{t,n}^{0}$ and then applying Equation~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-future-function-recursion}) successively gives
\begin{equation}
F_{t,n}
=
P_{t,t+1}^{(n-1)}P_{t+1,t+2}^{(n-1)}\cdots
P_{n-2,n-1}^{(n-1)}M_n(G_n),
\label{eq:one-lag-F-kernel-factorization}
\end{equation}
where the normalized kernels are those in Equation~
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-forward-normalized-kernel}); when $t=n-1$, the
product preceding $M_n(G_n)$ is empty.  Equation~
(\ref{eq:weak-bias-forward-minorization}) contracts the oscillation at
each displayed normalized kernel.  The same calculation applied to
$M_n$ itself gives
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{osc}\{M_n(G_n)\}
\leq
\left(1-\frac{\underbar M}{\bar M}\right)
\operatorname{osc}(G_n).
\end{equation}
Thus there is a $\varrho\in(0,1)$, independent of $s,t,n$, such that
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n})
\leq
C\varrho^{n-t}\operatorname{osc}(G_n)
\leq C\varrho^{n-t}.
\label{eq:one-lag-F-contraction}
\end{equation}

For clarity, we now prove the empirical-ratio estimate used in every
summand.  Fix $t$, a probability measure $\mu$, and normalize
$q_{t,n}^{0}$ so that $\mu(q_{t,n}^{0})=1$.  For any bounded $f$, define
\begin{equation}
\bar\mu(f)=\mu(q_{t,n}^{0}f),
\qquad
\bar\mu^J(f)=
\frac{\mu^J(q_{t,n}^{0}f)}{\mu^J(q_{t,n}^{0})}.
\label{eq:one-lag-weighted-empirical-measures}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
C_{t,n}(\mu)
=\frac{\bar\mu(h_{s,i}F_{t,n})}{\bar\mu(F_{t,n})}
-\bar\mu(h_{s,i}),
\label{eq:one-lag-C-weighted-form}
\end{equation}
and the same identity holds with $\mu$ replaced by $\mu^J$.  From
Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-weighted-empirical-measures}),
\begin{align}
\bar\mu^J(f)-\bar\mu(f)
&=
\frac{
(\mu^J-\mu)
\left[q_{t,n}^{0}\{f-\bar\mu(f)\}\right]
}{\mu^J(q_{t,n}^{0})}.
\label{eq:one-lag-weighted-ratio-centered}
\end{align}
Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-q-zero-ratio}) makes the denominator uniformly
positive after this normalization and gives
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{osc}
\left[q_{t,n}^{0}\{f-\bar\mu(f)\}\right]
\leq2R\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:one-lag-weighted-centered-oscillation}
\end{equation}
Thus, if $\mu^J$ is an empirical measure of $J$ conditionally
independent draws from $\mu$, the fourth-power calculation in Equation~
(\ref{eq:conditional-filter-resampling-fourth-moment}) gives
\begin{equation}
\left\|\bar\mu^J(f)-\bar\mu(f)\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{C}{\sqrt J}\operatorname{osc}(f).
\label{eq:one-lag-weighted-empirical-l4}
\end{equation}
At $t=s$, $h_{s,i}$ is a function of the current time-$s$ particle.
The four functions
\begin{equation}
q_{s,n}^{0},\qquad
q_{s,n}^{0}h_{s,i},\qquad
q_{s,n}^{0}F_{s,n},\qquad
q_{s,n}^{0}h_{s,i}F_{s,n}
\label{eq:one-lag-base-current-functions}
\end{equation}
are therefore bounded deterministic functions of that current particle.
Apply Equation~(\ref{eq:karjalainen-predictor-l4}) separately to their
centered versions.  Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-q-zero-ratio}) bounds the
oscillation of $q_{s,n}^{0}$ after normalization and bounds
$m(\xi_s^P)(q_{s,n}^{0})$ away from zero.  Substitution into
Equations~(\ref{eq:one-lag-weighted-ratio-centered}) and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-covariance-difference}) then gives Equation~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-weighted-empirical-l4}) at $t=s$.  Thus Karjalainen et
al.'s result is used only when $h_{s,i}$ is current; no historical
function at a later time is inserted into that result.

Choose constants $h_0$ and $F_0$ in the respective ranges of $h_{s,i}$
and $F_{t,n}$, and put
\begin{equation}
A(\nu)
=
\nu\{(h_{s,i}-h_0)(F_{t,n}-F_0)\}
-\nu(h_{s,i}-h_0)\nu(F_{t,n}-F_0).
\label{eq:one-lag-covariance-functional}
\end{equation}
Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-C-weighted-form}) is equivalently
\begin{equation}
C_{t,n}(\mu)=\frac{A(\bar\mu)}{\bar\mu(F_{t,n})}.
\label{eq:one-lag-C-covariance-form}
\end{equation}
The difference of the two covariance numerators satisfies
\begin{align}
|A(\bar\mu^J)-A(\bar\mu)|
&\leq
\left|
(\bar\mu^J-\bar\mu)
\{(h_{s,i}-h_0)(F_{t,n}-F_0)\}
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\quad+
\left|
(\bar\mu^J-\bar\mu)(h_{s,i}-h_0)
\right|
\left|\bar\mu^J(F_{t,n}-F_0)\right|                       \notag\\
&\quad+
\left|\bar\mu(h_{s,i}-h_0)\right|
\left|
(\bar\mu^J-\bar\mu)(F_{t,n}-F_0)
\right|.
\label{eq:one-lag-covariance-difference}
\end{align}
Equations~(\ref{eq:one-lag-weighted-empirical-l4}) and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-covariance-difference}), together with
\begin{align}
\|h_{s,i}-h_0\|_\infty
&\leq\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i}),                           \notag\\
\|F_{t,n}-F_0\|_\infty
&\leq\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n}),                           \notag\\
\operatorname{osc}\{(h_{s,i}-h_0)(F_{t,n}-F_0)\}
&\leq2\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})
\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n}),
\end{align}
give
\begin{equation}
\left\|A(\bar\mu^J)-A(\bar\mu)\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{C}{\sqrt J}
\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n}).
\label{eq:one-lag-covariance-l4}
\end{equation}
Finally, Equations~(\ref{eq:one-lag-F-bounds}) and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-C-covariance-form}) give
\begin{align}
&\left\|C_{t,n}(\mu^J)-C_{t,n}(\mu)\right\|_{L^4}          \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac1{\underbar G}
\left\|A(\bar\mu^J)-A(\bar\mu)\right\|_{L^4}
+\frac{\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})
\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n})}{\underbar G^2}
\left\|\bar\mu^J(F_{t,n})-\bar\mu(F_{t,n})\right\|_{L^4}
                                                                    \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{C}{\sqrt J}
\operatorname{osc}(h_{s,i})\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n}).
\label{eq:one-lag-continuation-local-l4}
\end{align}
In the last line, a second factor $\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n})$ arising
from the reciprocal-denominator term is absorbed into $C$ using
$\operatorname{osc}(F_{t,n})\leq\bar G-\underbar G$.

Conditionally on the time-$(t-1)$ prediction paths,
$m(\xi_t^P)$ is the empirical measure of $J$ independent draws from
$\Phi_t\{m(\xi_{t-1}^P)\}$.  Equations~
(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}),
(\ref{eq:one-lag-F-contraction}), and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-continuation-local-l4}) therefore give
\begin{align}
&\left\|
C_{t,n}\{m(\xi_t^P)\}
-C_{t,n}\left\{\Phi_t(m(\xi_{t-1}^P))\right\}
\right\|_{L^4}                                             \notag\\
&\qquad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}\varrho^{n-t},
\qquad s<t\leq n.
\label{eq:one-lag-local-telescope-l4}
\end{align}
The time-$s$ version, using Karjalainen et al.'s prediction bound as
specified after Equation~(\ref{eq:one-lag-weighted-empirical-l4}), is
\begin{equation}
\left\|
C_{s,n}\{m(\xi_s^P)\}-C_{s,n}(\eta_s)
\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}\varrho^{n-s}.
\label{eq:one-lag-base-telescope-l4}
\end{equation}
Minkowski's inequality, Equations~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-continuation-telescope})--
(\ref{eq:one-lag-base-telescope-l4}), and the finite geometric sum give
\begin{align}
&\left\|
C_{n,n}\{m(\xi_n^P)\}-C_{n,n}(\eta_n)
\right\|_{L^4}                                             \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}
\sum_{t=s}^{n}\varrho^{n-t}                               \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J(1-\varrho)}.
\label{eq:one-lag-predictable-l4}
\end{align}
Absorbing the fixed factor $(1-\varrho)^{-1}$ into $C$, Equations~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-resampling-l4}),
(\ref{eq:one-lag-continuation-endpoints}), and
(\ref{eq:one-lag-predictable-l4}) prove
\begin{equation}
\left\|\Delta_{n,r,i}^J-\Delta_{n,r,i}\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J},
\qquad1\leq r<n.
\label{eq:one-lag-particle-error-l4}
\end{equation}
Applying Equation~(\ref{eq:l4-centering-bridge}) with deterministic
target $\Delta_{n,r,i}$ gives
\begin{equation}
\left\|\Delta_{n,r,i}^J-\E\Delta_{n,r,i}^J\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J},
\qquad1\leq r<n.
\label{eq:one-lag-centered-l4}
\end{equation}
For $r=0$, substitute the current function $h_{n,i}$ into Equation~
(\ref{eq:filtered-particle-l4}), use Equation~
(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}), and center by Equation~
(\ref{eq:l4-centering-bridge}).  This gives
\begin{equation}
\left\|\Delta_{n,0,i}^J-\E\Delta_{n,0,i}^J\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}.
\label{eq:current-lag-centered-l4}
\end{equation}

Take coordinate $i$ in Equation~
(\ref{eq:conditional-score-path-decomposition}), center both sides, and
apply Minkowski's inequality and Equations~
(\ref{eq:one-lag-centered-l4})--
(\ref{eq:current-lag-centered-l4}).  Then
\begin{align}
&\left\|
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
-\E\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\|_{L^4}                                               \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}
\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}\alpha^r                                   \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J(1-\alpha)}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-centered-l4-direct}
\end{align}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
\Var\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J(1-\alpha)^2}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-one-time-variance-direct}
\end{equation}

We next calculate the mixing coefficient used to sum the conditional
scores.  Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-weight-difference}) gives
the two measurable inclusions
\begin{align}
\sigma\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_1^\alpha(\theta),\ldots,
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_m^\alpha(\theta)
\right\}
&\subseteq
\sigma\{X_{0,1:J}^P,\ldots,X_{m+1,1:J}^P\},
\label{eq:past-score-sigma-field-inclusion}
\\
\sigma\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_{m+l}^\alpha(\theta),
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_{m+l+1}^\alpha(\theta),\ldots
\right\}
&\subseteq
\sigma\{X_{m+l,1:J}^P,X_{m+l+1,1:J}^P,\ldots\}.
\label{eq:future-score-sigma-field-inclusion}
\end{align}
In these displays $X_{t,j}^P$ is the differentiable prediction particle
used throughout this section, including its current simulator and weight
gradients.  No ancestral path coordinate is added to either sigma-field.
Let $A$ and $B$ belong respectively to the sigma-fields on the left of
Equations~(\ref{eq:past-score-sigma-field-inclusion}) and
(\ref{eq:future-score-sigma-field-inclusion}).  Let
$\mathcal F_{m+1}^P$ be the sigma-field generated by all variables through
the construction of $X_{m+1,1:J}^P$.  Then
\begin{equation}
\Pr(B\mid\mathcal F_{m+1}^P)
=
K(X_{m+1,1:J}^P,B)
\label{eq:future-score-conditional-kernel}
\end{equation}
for the Markov kernel from the time-$(m+1)$ prediction cloud to the
future event $B$.  Since $1_A$ is $\mathcal F_{m+1}^P$-measurable,
Equation~(\ref{eq:future-score-conditional-kernel}) gives
\begin{align}
&\left|\Pr(A\cap B)-\Pr(A)\Pr(B)\right|                     \notag\\
&\quad=
\left|
\E\left[
1_A\left\{
K(X_{m+1,1:J}^P,B)-\E K(X_{m+1,1:J}^P,B)
\right\}
\right]
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\sup_{\mathbf x,\mathbf x'}
\left|K(\mathbf x,B)-K(\mathbf x',B)\right|                \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\beta_{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\textbf{M}_{m+1,m+l}\right)       \notag\\
&\quad\leq
(1-\epsilon)^{\left\lfloor
(l-1)/[c\{1+\log J\}]
\right\rfloor},
\qquad l\geq2,
\label{eq:score-event-mixing-calculation}
\end{align}
where the last inequality is Theorem~3.1 of
\citet{karjalainen23}.  Taking the supremum over $m,A$, and $B$ in
Equation~(\ref{eq:score-event-mixing-calculation}) gives
\begin{equation}
a(l)
\leq
(1-\epsilon)^{\left\lfloor
(l-1)/[c\{1+\log J\}]
\right\rfloor},
\qquad l\geq2.
\label{eq:conditional-score-direct-alpha-mixing}
\end{equation}
For $l=1$, $a(1)\leq1$.

For $m<n$, Davydov's inequality and Equation~
(\ref{eq:conditional-score-centered-l4-direct}) give
\begin{align}
&\left|
\Cov\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_m^\alpha(\theta),
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\}
\right|                                                       \notag\\
&\qquad\leq
C a(n-m)^{1/2}
\left\|
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_m^\alpha(
\theta)-\E\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_m^\alpha(\theta)
\right\|_{L^4}                                               \notag\\
&\hspace{42mm}\times
\left\|
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(
\theta)-\E\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\|_{L^4}                                               \notag\\
&\qquad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J(1-\alpha)^2}
a(n-m)^{1/2}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-direct-davydov}
\end{align}
Put $q=(1-\epsilon)^{1/2}<1$.  Summing the blocked geometric series in
Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-direct-alpha-mixing}) gives
\begin{align}
\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}a(l)^{1/2}
&\leq
1+
\sum_{l=2}^{N-1}
q^{\left\lfloor(l-1)/[c\{1+\log J\}]\right\rfloor}         \notag\\
&\leq
\min\left\{
N-1,
1+\left\lceil c\{1+\log J\}\right\rceil
\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}q^u
\right\}                                                     \notag\\
&=
\min\left\{
N-1,
1+\frac{\left\lceil c\{1+\log J\}\right\rceil}{1-q}
\right\}                                                     \notag\\
&\leq
C\{1+\min(N,1+\log J)\}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-direct-mixing-sum}
\end{align}
Moreover, collecting the pairs $(m,n)$ having the same lag $l=n-m$
gives the finite identity
\begin{align}
\sum_{1\leq m<n\leq N}a(n-m)^{1/2}
&=
\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}
\sum_{m=1}^{N-l}a(l)^{1/2}                                  \notag\\
&=
\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}(N-l)a(l)^{1/2}                             \notag\\
&\leq
N\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}a(l)^{1/2}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-pair-count}
\end{align}
Equations~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-one-time-variance-direct})--
(\ref{eq:conditional-score-pair-count}) now imply
\begin{align}
&\Var\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)
\right\}                                                     \notag\\
&\quad=
\sum_{n=1}^{N}
\Var\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\}
+2\sum_{1\leq m<n\leq N}
\Cov\left\{
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_m^\alpha(\theta),
\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\}                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\sum_{n=1}^{N}
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J(1-\alpha)^2}
+
2\sum_{1\leq m<n\leq N}
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J(1-\alpha)^2}a(n-m)^{1/2}            \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CNG'(\theta)^2}{J(1-\alpha)^2}
\left\{1+\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}a(l)^{1/2}\right\}              \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CNG'(\theta)^2}{J(1-\alpha)^2}
\{1+\min(N,1+\log J)\}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-direct-coordinate-variance}
\end{align}
The manuscript's $\lesssim$ convention suppresses the last mixing-time
factor.  Finally, sum Equation~
(\ref{eq:conditional-score-direct-coordinate-variance}) over
$i=1,\ldots,p$.  This proves Equation~
(\ref{eq:refined-dmop-alpha-variance}).
\end{proof}
\fi

\iffalse
Applying Equations~(\ref{eq:karjalainen-predictor-l4}) and
(\ref{eq:filtered-particle-l4}) to the two gradient functions, and using
$\operatorname{osc}(f)\leq2\|f\|_\infty$, gives
\begin{align}
&\left\|
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^{J}
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n,j}^{F,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
-
\E\left[
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^{J}
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n,j}^{F,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
\right]
\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}
\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}\alpha^r,
\label{eq:filtered-weight-centered-l4}
\\
&\left\|
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^{J}
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n,j}^{P,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
-
\E\left[
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^{J}
\left.\partial_{\theta_i}\log w_{n,j}^{P,\theta}
\right|_{\theta=\phi}
\right]
\right\|_{L^4}
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}
\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}\alpha^r.
\label{eq:prediction-weight-centered-l4}
\end{align}
We now use Equation~(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}) before
summing over time.  By Equation~(\ref{eq:unrolled-filtered-weight}), the
$i$th coordinate of
$m(\xi_t)(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-k}h_k)$ is precisely the filtered
weight-gradient average in Equation~
(\ref{eq:filtered-weight-centered-l4}). Therefore
\begin{align}
&\left\|
(1-\alpha)
\left[
m(\xi_t)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-k}h_{k,i}\right)
-\E m(\xi_t)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right]
\right\|_{L^4}                                                \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}
(1-\alpha)\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-k}                         \notag\\
&\quad=
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}(1-\alpha^t),
\qquad 1\leq t<N,
\label{eq:interior-full-score-centered-l4}
\\
&\left\|
m(\xi_N)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}h_{k,i}\right)
-\E m(\xi_N)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right\|_{L^4}                                                \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)}{\sqrt J}
\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}.
\label{eq:terminal-full-score-centered-l4}
\end{align}
The identities in the last lines are finite-sum identities and remain
valid at $\alpha=1$.

Each interior summand in Equation~
(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}) is a measurable function of the
current particle-location cloud. Theorem~3.1 of
\citet{karjalainen23} applies to this particle-cloud chain. We spell out
the passage from its Dobrushin bound to the mixing coefficient. Let $A$
be an event generated by these current-cloud functionals through time
$m$, let $B$ be an event generated by the functionals from time $m+l$
onward, and let $\mathcal F_{m+1}^P$ be the sigma-field generated by all
variables through the production of the prediction cloud
$X_{m+1,1:J}^P$. The time-$m$ resampling indices enter both the time-$m$
functional and the construction of $X_{m+1,1:J}^P$, so
$A\in\mathcal F_{m+1}^P$. Conditional on this sigma-field, the Markov
property gives, for a suitable future-tail kernel $K$,
\begin{equation}
\Pr(B\mid\mathcal F_{m+1}^P)
=
K(X_{m+1,1:J}^P,B).
\label{eq:score-future-markov-kernel}
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{align}
&\left|\Pr(A\cap B)-\Pr(A)\Pr(B)\right|                     \notag\\
&\quad=
\left|
\E\left[
1_A\left\{
K(X_{m+1,1:J}^P,B)-\E K(X_{m+1,1:J}^P,B)
\right\}
\right]
\right|                                                     \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\sup_{\mathbf x,\mathbf x'}
\left|K(\mathbf x,B)-K(\mathbf x',B)\right|                \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\beta_{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\textbf{M}_{m+1,m+l}\right).
\label{eq:score-events-dobrushin-bound}
\end{align}
The kernel from the prediction cloud at time $m+1$ to the prediction
cloud at time $m+l$ has $l-1$ particle-filter steps; postcomposition with
terminal resampling and the future-tail kernel cannot increase its
Dobrushin coefficient. Passing to a measurable functional can only reduce
the generated $\sigma$-algebra. Therefore,
for some $\epsilon\in(0,1)$ and $c<\infty$ depending only on the bounds
in Assumptions~{\refAssumpBoundedProcess}--
{\refAssumpBoundedMeasurement}, Theorem~3.1 gives, for $l\geq2$,
\begin{equation}
a(l)
\leq
(1-\epsilon)^{\left\lfloor
(l-1)/[c\{1+\log J\}]\right\rfloor}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-alpha-mixing}
\end{equation}
For $l=1$, the same display follows from $a(1)\leq1$. For $m<n<N$,
Davydov's inequality, applied to the centered interior summands in
Equation~(\ref{eq:interior-full-score-centered-l4}), gives
\begin{align}
&\left|
\Cov\left(
(1-\alpha)m(\xi_m)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha^{m-k}h_{k,i}\right),
(1-\alpha)m(\xi_n)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\alpha^{n-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right)
\right|                                                       \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
a(n-m)^{1/2}
(1-\alpha^m)(1-\alpha^n)                                    \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{2J}
a(n-m)^{1/2}
\left\{
(1-\alpha^m)^2+(1-\alpha^n)^2
\right\},
\label{eq:interior-full-score-davydov-covariance}
\end{align}
where the last line uses $2xy\leq x^2+y^2$.  Equation~
(\ref{eq:interior-full-score-centered-l4}) also gives
\begin{equation}
\Var\left(
(1-\alpha)m(\xi_n)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\alpha^{n-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right)
\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
(1-\alpha^n)^2.
\label{eq:interior-full-score-one-time-variance}
\end{equation}

Put $q=(1-\epsilon)^{1/2}<1$.  Directly summing the blocked geometric
series in Equation~(\ref{eq:conditional-score-alpha-mixing}) gives
\begin{align}
\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}a(l)^{1/2}
&\leq
\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}
q^{\left\lfloor(l-1)/[c\{1+\log J\}]\right\rfloor}         \notag\\
&\leq
\min\left\{
N-1,
\lceil c\{1+\log J\}\rceil\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}q^u
\right\}                                                     \notag\\
&\leq
C\{1+\min(N,\log J)\}.
\label{eq:conditional-score-mixing-sum}
\end{align}
Expanding the variance of the interior sum, using Equations~
(\ref{eq:interior-full-score-davydov-covariance})--
(\ref{eq:conditional-score-mixing-sum}), and observing that for each lag
$l$ every squared term occurs at most twice, we obtain
\begin{align}
&\Var\left(
(1-\alpha)\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}
m(\xi_t)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right)                                                       \notag\\
&\quad=
\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}
\Var\left(
(1-\alpha)m(\xi_n)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\alpha^{n-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right)
\notag\\
&\qquad
+2\sum_{1\leq m<n<N}
\Cov\left(
\begin{aligned}
&(1-\alpha)m(\xi_m)
\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha^{m-k}h_{k,i}\right),\\
&(1-\alpha)m(\xi_n)
\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\alpha^{n-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\end{aligned}
\right)                                                       \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^n)^2
+
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}a(l)^{1/2}
\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^n)^2                              \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\{1+\min(N,1+\log J)\}
\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^n)^2.
\label{eq:interior-full-score-variance-with-mixing-time}
\end{align}
Equation~(\ref{eq:terminal-full-score-centered-l4}) gives separately
\begin{align}
&\Var\left\{
m(\xi_N)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}h_{k,i}\right)
\right\}                                                       \notag\\
&\quad\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}\right)^2.
\label{eq:terminal-full-score-variance}
\end{align}
For any square-integrable random variables $U$ and $V$,
\begin{align}
\Var(U+V)
&=\E\{(U-\E U)+(V-\E V)\}^2                              \notag\\
&\leq
2\Var(U)+2\Var(V),
\label{eq:two-term-variance-bound}
\end{align}
where the inequality follows from $(x+y)^2\leq2x^2+2y^2$.
Apply Equation~(\ref{eq:two-term-variance-bound}) to the two terms of
Equation~(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}), and then use
Equations~(\ref{eq:interior-full-score-variance-with-mixing-time}) and
(\ref{eq:terminal-full-score-variance}). This gives
\begin{align}
\Var\left\{\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right\}
&\leq
\frac{CG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\{1+\min(N,1+\log J)\}                                    \notag\\
&\qquad\times
\left\{
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)^2
+\left(\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r\right)^2
\right\}.
\label{eq:full-score-variance-with-mixing-time}
\end{align}
Summing Equation~(\ref{eq:full-score-variance-with-mixing-time}) over
the $p$ coordinates and using the convention that $\lesssim$ suppresses
the displayed particle-system mixing-time factor proves the stochastic
term in Equation~(\ref{eq:dmop-alpha-current-particle-variance}). Since
$(1-\alpha^t)^2\leq1$ for $\alpha\in[0,1]$,
\begin{equation}
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)^2
\leq N-1\leq N.
\label{eq:interior-full-score-sum-bound}
\end{equation}
Equations~(\ref{eq:full-score-variance-with-mixing-time}) and
(\ref{eq:interior-full-score-sum-bound}), together with the finite
geometric sum in the statement of the lemma, prove Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-alpha-n-over-j-variance}).

It remains to obtain the other term in the minimum.  Taking absolute
values in Equation~(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}) and using
Equation~(\ref{eq:discounted-score-supremum}) give, pathwise,
\begin{align}
\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right|
&\leq
G'(\theta)
\left[
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)
+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-k}
\right]                                                       \notag\\
&=
G'(\theta)
\left[
(N-1)-\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\alpha^t
+1+\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\alpha^t
\right]                                                       \notag\\
&=
NG'(\theta).
\label{eq:full-score-deterministic-bound}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\begin{align}
\Var\left(\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right)
&\leq
\E\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right|^2
\leq
N^2G'(\theta)^2.
\end{align}
Combining this deterministic bound with Equation~
(\ref{eq:full-score-variance-with-mixing-time}), summing over
coordinates, and applying the $\lesssim$ convention proves Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-alpha-current-particle-variance}).
\end{proof}
\fi

\iffalse
\begin{remark}[Exact neutral-model check]
Suppose $J\geq2$, $\theta=\phi$, $\alpha\in[0,1]$, Assumptions
{\refAssumpBoundedProcess}, {\refAssumpBoundedMeasurement}, and
{\refAssumpDifferentiableSimulator} hold, and multinomial resampling is
used.  Then
\begin{equation}
\Var\left\{\nabla_\theta\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right\}
\leq
N^2pG'(\theta)^2.
\label{eq:dmop-alpha-deterministic-variance-bound}
\end{equation}
In general, no multiplicative constant independent of $N,J$, and
$\alpha$ makes
\begin{equation}
\frac{pG'(\theta)^2}{J}
\left[
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)^2
+\left(\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r\right)^2
\right]
\label{eq:withdrawn-current-cloud-variance-bound}
\end{equation}
an upper bound for the variance.  In particular, there is a model
satisfying the assumptions of this lemma for which
\begin{align}
\Var\left\{\nabla_\theta\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right\}
&=
\frac{\sigma^2}{J}
\left[
N(2-J^{-1})
+\sum_{r=1}^{N-1}(N-r)\alpha^{2r}(1-J^{-1})^{r+1}
\right],
\label{eq:neutral-dmop-alpha-exact-variance}
\end{align}
where $\sigma^2>0$.  Consequently, before the geometric sum saturates
at the horizon, the genealogy contribution in this example has order
\begin{equation}
\frac{N}{J(1-\alpha^2)},
\label{eq:neutral-dmop-alpha-effective-memory}
\end{equation}
whereas Equation~
(\ref{eq:withdrawn-current-cloud-variance-bound}) has order
$J^{-1}\{N+(1-\alpha)^{-2}\}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}[Proof.]
Equation~(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}) and
Equation~(\ref{eq:bounded-realized-measurement-score}) give, for every
coordinate $i$,
\begin{align}
\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right|
&\leq
G'(\theta)
\left[
(1-\alpha)\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\alpha^{t-s}
+\sum_{s=1}^{N}\alpha^{N-s}
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&=
G'(\theta)
\left[
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)
+\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r
\right]                                                     \notag\\
&=
NG'(\theta).
\label{eq:dmop-alpha-coordinate-pathwise-bound}
\end{align}
The finite-sum identity in the last line holds directly at
$\alpha=1$.  Therefore
\begin{align}
\Var\left\{\nabla_\theta\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right\}
&=
\sum_{i=1}^{p}
\Var\left\{\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right\}
                                                               \notag\\
&\leq
\sum_{i=1}^{p}
\E\left|\partial_{\theta_i}\hat\ell^\alpha(\theta)\right|^2
                                                               \notag\\
&\leq
N^2pG'(\theta)^2,
\end{align}
which proves Equation~
(\ref{eq:dmop-alpha-deterministic-variance-bound}).

We now construct and calculate the counterexample.  Let the state space
be $[0,1]$, let
\begin{equation}
X_n=U_n,
\qquad
U_n\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}{\rm Uniform}[0,1],
\end{equation}
and let $Y_n\in\{0,1\}$.  In a neighborhood of $\phi=0$, set
\begin{equation}
\Pr_\theta(Y_n=1\mid X_n=x)
=
\frac12+\frac{\theta}{4}(2x-1),
\end{equation}
and fix $y_n^*=1$ for every $n$.  The transition density is identically
one, the simulator is infinitely differentiable, and the two measurement
probabilities and their total derivatives are bounded above and away from
zero on a sufficiently small neighborhood of zero.  Hence Assumptions
{\refAssumpBoundedProcess}, {\refAssumpBoundedMeasurement}, and
{\refAssumpDifferentiableSimulator} hold.  At $\theta=\phi=0$ the
measurement weights are constant and
\begin{align}
h_n
&=
\left.
\partial_\theta\log
\Pr_\theta(Y_n=1\mid X_n)
\right|_{\theta=0}
=
\frac{2X_n-1}{2},                                           \notag\\
\E(h_n)&=0,
&
\sigma^2:=\E(h_n^2)&=\frac1{12}.
\label{eq:neutral-model-score-moments}
\end{align}

Fix an injection time $s$.  Only within this calculation, let
$M_{s,0}$ be the empirical mean of the $J$ fresh values of $h_s$ before
the time-$s$ resampling, and let $M_{s,d}$ be their descendant mean after
$d$ neutral multinomial selections.  Put
\begin{equation}
e_{s,d}=M_{s,d}-M_{s,d-1},
\qquad d\geq1.
\end{equation}
The sequence $(M_{s,d})_{d\geq0}$ is a martingale with respect to the
successive resampling sigma-fields.  Consequently its increments are
orthogonal.  We next compute all their variances rather than invoking an
asymptotic approximation.  Since a uniformly selected descendant has the
same marginal distribution as its ancestor,
\begin{equation}
\E\left\{
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^{J}h_{s,j}^2
\right\}
=\sigma^2
\end{equation}
at every selection generation.  Conditional multinomial sampling gives
\begin{align}
\E(e_{s,d}^2\mid M_{s,d-1},h_{s,1:J}^{(d-1)})
&=
\frac1J
\left\{
\frac1J\sum_{j=1}^{J}(h_{s,j}^{(d-1)})^2
-M_{s,d-1}^2
\right\}.
\label{eq:neutral-selection-increment-conditional-variance}
\end{align}
Writing $V_d=\Var(M_{s,d})$, taking expectations in Equation~
(\ref{eq:neutral-selection-increment-conditional-variance}), and using
the orthogonality of $M_{s,d-1}$ and $e_{s,d}$ yield
\begin{align}
V_d-V_{d-1}
&=
\frac{\sigma^2-V_{d-1}}{J},                                \notag\\
V_d
&=
(1-J^{-1})V_{d-1}+\frac{\sigma^2}{J},
\qquad
V_0=\frac{\sigma^2}{J}.
\label{eq:neutral-selection-variance-recursion}
\end{align}
Solving the scalar recursion gives
\begin{align}
V_d
&=
\sigma^2\{1-(1-J^{-1})^{d+1}\},                            \notag\\
\Var(e_{s,d})
&=
V_d-V_{d-1}
=
\frac{\sigma^2}{J}(1-J^{-1})^d.
\label{eq:neutral-selection-increment-variance}
\end{align}

Put $L=N-s$.  The summation-by-parts identity shows that the full-score
contribution injected at time $s$ is
\begin{align}
&(1-\alpha)\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\alpha^rM_{s,r+1}
+\alpha^LM_{s,L+1}                                        \notag\\
&\quad=
M_{s,1}
+\sum_{r=1}^{L}\alpha^r(M_{s,r+1}-M_{s,r})                \notag\\
&\quad=
M_{s,0}+e_{s,1}
+\sum_{r=1}^{L}\alpha^re_{s,r+1}.
\label{eq:neutral-one-injection-martingale-decomposition}
\end{align}
The first equality is the contribution of $h_s$ in Equation~
(\ref{eq:full-score-summation-by-parts}); the second follows by
collecting the coefficient of each $M_{s,d}$; and the third uses the
definition of $e_{s,d}$.  Orthogonality and Equation~
(\ref{eq:neutral-selection-increment-variance}) therefore give
\begin{align}
&\Var\left\{
(1-\alpha)\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\alpha^rM_{s,r+1}
+\alpha^LM_{s,L+1}
\right\}                                                   \notag\\
&\quad=
\frac{\sigma^2}{J}
\left[
1+(1-J^{-1})
+\sum_{r=1}^{L}\alpha^{2r}(1-J^{-1})^{r+1}
\right].
\label{eq:neutral-one-injection-exact-variance}
\end{align}

Conditional on all resampling indices, the contributions injected at
different times are linear functions of independent centered collections
of fresh variables $h_s$.  Their conditional cross-products therefore
have expectation zero, and hence their unconditional covariances are
zero.  Summing Equation~
(\ref{eq:neutral-one-injection-exact-variance}) over $s=1,\ldots,N$,
and observing that the summand indexed by $r$ occurs for exactly $N-r$
values of $s$, proves Equation~
(\ref{eq:neutral-dmop-alpha-exact-variance}).

Finally, take $N=m^2$, $J=m^3$, and $\alpha=1-m^{-1}$.  Then
\begin{align}
&\frac{\sigma^2}{J}
\sum_{r=1}^{N-1}
(N-r)\alpha^{2r}(1-J^{-1})^{r+1}
\longrightarrow
\frac{\sigma^2}{2},                                       \notag\\
&\frac1J
\left[
\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}(1-\alpha^t)^2
+\left(\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\alpha^r\right)^2
\right]
\leq
\frac{2N}{J}
=\frac2m
\longrightarrow0.
\end{align}
The first limit follows by dividing the geometric sum by $m$ and using
$(1-m^{-1})^{2\lfloor mx\rfloor}\to e^{-2x}$, while
$(N-r)/N\to1$ over the $r=O(m)$ terms carrying nonnegligible mass.  Thus
no multiplicative constant independent of $N,J$, and $\alpha$ can make
Equation~(\ref{eq:withdrawn-current-cloud-variance-bound}) a valid
upper bound.  The
same exact sum has order $N/\{J(1-\alpha^2)\}$ whenever
$1\ll(1-\alpha)^{-1}\ll\min(N,J)$, which proves Equation~
(\ref{eq:neutral-dmop-alpha-effective-memory}).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[DMOP-$0$ and the fixed-lag calculation]
The DMOP-$0$ argument given before Theorem~
\ref{thm:dmop-alpha-truncation-mse} remains valid.  With the manuscript's
convention that $\lesssim$ suppresses the particle-system mixing-time
factor, it gives
\begin{equation}
\Var\left(\nabla_\theta\hat\ell^0(\theta)\right)
\lesssim
\frac{NpG'(\theta)^2}{J},
\end{equation}
and Equation~(\ref{eq:neutral-dmop-alpha-exact-variance}) has the same
order at $\alpha=0$.  Moreover,
$(1-\alpha^2)^{-1}\leq(1-\alpha)^{-2}$ for
$0\leq\alpha<1$.  Thus the exact calculation refutes the former
summation-by-parts upper bound while remaining consistent with Lemma~
\ref{lem:dmop-alpha-variance}; the direct conditional-score proof has
the $N/J$ order exhibited by the example for every fixed $\alpha<1$.
\end{remark}
\fi

\begin{lem}[Population discounting bias]
\label{lem:dmop-population-discount-bias}
Under the conditions of Lemma~\ref{lem:derivative-measure-forgetting},
suppose $\theta=\phi$ and $\alpha\in[0,1]$. Then
\begin{align}
\left\|
\nabla_\theta\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
-\nabla_\theta\ell_n(\theta)
\right\|_2
&\leq
C\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)
\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}(1-\alpha^r)\varrho^r                       \notag\\
&\leq
C\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)
\frac{\varrho(1-\alpha)}
{(1-\varrho)(1-\alpha\varrho)}.
\label{eq:dmop-population-discount-bias}
\end{align}
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}[Proof.]
\medskip\noindent\emph{Step 1: exact lagwise difference.}\quad
Equation~(\ref{eq:population-conditional-score-path-decomposition}) at
$\alpha$ and at $\alpha=1$ gives
\begin{align}
\nabla_\theta\ell_n(\theta)
-\nabla_\theta\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
&=
\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}(1-\alpha^r)\Delta_{n,r}                     \notag\\
&=
\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}(1-\alpha^r)\Delta_{n,r},
\label{eq:population-discount-lag-difference}
\end{align}
because $1-\alpha^0=0$.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Step 2: apply forgetting and sum.}\quad
Taking norms, applying the triangle
inequality and Lemma~\ref{lem:derivative-measure-forgetting}, and then
extending the nonnegative scalar sum to infinity give
\begin{align}
\left\|
\nabla_\theta\ell_n(\theta)
-\nabla_\theta\ell_n^\alpha(\theta)
\right\|_2
&\leq
\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}(1-\alpha^r)\|\Delta_{n,r}\|_2              \notag\\
&\leq
C\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)
\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}(1-\alpha^r)\varrho^r                       \notag\\
&\leq
C\sqrt p\,G'(\theta)
